## Goal
Build a mobile‑first book‑sorting MVP that allows DJ’s wife to review floor photos of kids' books (coloring, activity, sticker), mark each as keep or discard, and export keep/discard lists. Host it privately on Railway with a secret link, as a dedicated service separate from HealthOS.

## Constraints & Preferences
- **Deploy target:** Railway (chief‑cloud project) — but NOT inside HealthOS; must be its own service/repo.
- **Access:** private, secret‑link (no public browsing).
- **Photo handling:** upload one or more wide floor photos; no need to store original images.
- **Detection:** best‑effort automatic detection, with a manual review step. Duplicates share the same decision.
- **UI:** mobile‑first, photo‑gallery grid (3×3 or responsive), green check / red X corner buttons, tap centre to enlarge. Category auto‑suggested. Tapping a duplicate toggles all copies.
- **Output:** keep list, discard list. Cards show: red X = discard, green check = keep, blank = undecided.
- **Code management:** must use GitHub; no editing in ephemeral VM filesystem alone; remove local scratch files after deployment.
- **Separation of projects:** Book Sorter must be a separate GitHub repo and a separate Railway service, distinct from HealthOS.
- **Cover rotation:** User expects each book’s cover text to be readable upright; manual per‑book rotation controls are now available.
- **Mobile layout:** Header must be compact; intro paragraph and hint box hidden on small screens; summary row hidden on mobile.
- **Decision buttons:** green check and red X should be transparent with colored outlines, not solid filled circles.

```text
It was a sync-key bug, not Sandra’s phone.

What was happening:
- Sandra’s edits **were saved on the server** — I verified **30 decisions** were there.
- But the app was matching decisions by a browser-computed image hash.
- Those hashes can differ across devices/browsers.
- Her phone saw the edits because it had its own matching local/browser state.
- Your browser computed different hashes, so it didn’t match most of her saved decisions.

I fixed it.

What I changed:
- The app now syncs decisions by stable book IDs like `01-01`, `01-02`, etc., instead of browser-specific hashes.
- The contact sheet uses the same stable IDs.
- I migrated Sandra’s existing saved edits into stable IDs.

Current live state:
- **33 books**
- **30 decisions migrated**
  - **14 keep**
  - **16 discard**
  - **3 undecided**
- All rotations still **90° right**
- Sandra welcome page still removed

Verified production:
- review route has the stable-ID sync code
- contact sheet has the stable-ID lookup
- `/api/decisions` has 30 stable `01-xx` decisions
- manifest has 33 items and rotations `[90]`

Try reloading here:
https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c

If your browser still shows stale state, hard refresh once — but the underlying sync issue is fixed now.

Treat this as an asset/pipeline update unless DJ explicitly asks for a separate standalone project.\n\n1. Locate the existing working app repo/service.\n2. Verify the current live secret route and manifest.\n3. Save the new source photo into the existing expected input location, e.g. `sample-images/01.jpg`.\n4. Rerun the established crop generator.\n5. Inspect the generated crop contact sheet visually before claiming success.\n6. Update manifest titles/rotations for the new photo; remove stale title/rotation overrides from old photos.\n7. Remove stale crop/source folders that are no longer referenced by the manifest.\n8. Run local tests and route checks.\n9. Commit/push to GitHub.\n10. Deploy to Railway and verify the real live route, manifest, contact sheet, and at least one crop asset.\n\n## Rotation rule\n\nIf DJ says “all books need to be rotated 90 degrees to the right,” set every manifest item’s `rotation` to `90` and update regression tests to assert `{90}`. Verify production by fetching `/sample-crops/manifest.json` and checking the unique marker/count/rotation set.\n\n## Welcome/intro overlays\n\nIf DJ asks to remove a welcome page such as “Hello Sandra,” remove:\n\n- overlay HTML markup\n- overlay CSS\n- DOM references in JS\n- event listeners\n- tests that assert the overlay exists\n\nAdd/modify a regression test that asserts the welcome copy, overlay IDs, and event listener are absent.\n\n## Blocker discipline\n\nGitHub/Railway/repo connection/deploy/pipeline issues are not user blockers if credentials/access exist. Diagnose and solve the specific layer:\n\n- GitHub repo access\n- Railway API auth\n- Railway repo trigger connection\n- deployment state\n- app route/assets\n- crop/manifest pipeline\n\nOnly surface a blocker if external user action is genuinely required. If Railway has no repo trigger, manual deploy by exact commit SHA can still be enough to ship the change.\n\n## Live verification checklist\n\n- Secret review route returns 200.\n- Contact sheet route returns 200.\n- Admin route returns 200 if the app has activity tracking.\n- Manifest contains the expected `generatedBy` marker, item count, source image(s), and rotation set.\n- At least one crop asset returns image content.\n- Old welcome copy is absent if removed.\n- Saved decisions are reset only when the new item set should start clean.\n", "file_type": ".md"}

_tool_call_id: `call_kEjUZY7gWDb5WGIBv0hoKyKX`_

### assistant

```text
Right now: **we do not have a real historical archive.**

Current behavior:
- Shared selections live in one Railway volume file:
  - `/data/book-sorter/decisions.json`
- That file represents the **current active photo set**.
- Since item IDs are stable only within the current manifest (`01-01`, `01-02`, etc.), a new photo set would reuse IDs and the old selections would either:
  - need to be cleared, or
  - risk incorrectly applying to the new books.
- Activity history is separate in `/data/book-sorter/activity.json`, but that’s user/session/action metadata — not a durable archive of each completed selection set.

So practically: **today we dump/overwrite/clear old selections when a new photo comes in**, unless I manually export or snapshot them first.

What I’d recommend before we keep cycling through more photos:

1. Add a `sets` concept.
   - Example:
     - `setId: shelf-[REDACTED_PHONE]`
     - `setName: "Kids shelf round 1"`
     - `sourcePhoto: sample-01.jpg`
     - `createdAt`
     - manifest item count

2. Store decisions by set:
   - `/data/book-sorter/sets/shelf-[REDACTED_PHONE]/decisions.json`
   - `/data/book-sorter/sets/shelf-[REDACTED_PHONE]/manifest.json`
   - `/data/book-sorter/sets/shelf-[REDACTED_PHONE]/activity.json`

3. Keep a current pointer:
   - `/data/book-sorter/current-set.json`
   - The live app loads only the current set by default.

4. Add a simple archive/admin page:
   - list all past sets
   - counts: keep / discard / undecided
   - links to archived contact sheets
   - maybe export JSON/text

5. Before replacing photos, automatically snapshot the active set.
   - That gives us a durable record of “what Sandra/DJ decided for this batch” before generating the next one.

I’d make this change now before processing the next photo. It’s a small architecture cleanup and prevents accidental loss.
```

No Google Cloud project needed.\n  Load the himalaya skill and follow its setup instructions.\n\n- **Email + Calendar** → Continue with this skill, but use\n  `--services email,calendar` during auth so the consent screen only asks for\n  the scopes they actually need.\n\n- **Calendar/Drive/Sheets/Docs only** → Continue with this skill and use a\n  narrower `--services` set like `calendar,drive,sheets,docs`.\n\n- **Full Workspace access** → Continue with this skill and use the default\n  `all` service set.\n\n**Question 2: \"Does your Google account use Advanced Protection (hardware\nsecurity keys required to sign in)? ### assistant

```text
Done — I created the Google Sheet and wired the app so selections are append-logged going forward.

Google Sheet:
https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1Yurn6ACfQDHj-FbwRCmd6pbS_pyXFhTXMNBUJ8w0bOI/edit

What it now logs on every future selection change:
- Timestamp ET
- Timestamp UTC
- Reviewer
- Device
- Session ID
- Set ID
- Item ID
- Book title
- Category
- Decision: keep / discard / clear
- Rotation
- Source photo
- App URL

App behavior:
- The first time someone makes a selection on a device, it asks: **“Who is making this selection?”**
- That name is saved in that browser/device and sent with future decisions.
- Category/rotation-only edits do **not** append noise rows; only actual keep/discard/clear selection changes are logged.
- The app still keeps the live shared state in Railway as before.

Deployed:
- Commit: `23bc553`
- Railway deploy: successful
- Live app verified with the new logging code.
- Local tests: **23/23 passing**
- Google Sheets append path verified end-to-end, and the verification row was removed, so the sheet is clean with just headers right now.

Important note: prior selections don’t have reliable “who made it” data, so I did **not** backfill them into the Sheet as if we knew. Going forward, each selection will be cataloged with who/date.
```

uploaded=0; trashed=0; bytes_total=0\nfor idx,row in enumerate(values[1:], start=2):\n    if len(row) < 7: continue\n    item_id=row[6]; item=items.get(item_id)\n    if not item: continue\n    # Create 320px max thumbnail, JPEG quality 55.\n    crop_url=BASE+item['src']\n    raw=urllib.request.urlopen(crop_url, timeout=30).read()\n    im=Image.open(io.BytesIO(raw)).convert('RGB')\n    im.thumbnail((320,320), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)\n    out=io.BytesIO(); im.save(out, format='JPEG', quality=55, optimize=True, progressive=True)\n    data=out.getvalue(); bytes_total += len(data)\n    set_id=row[5] if len(row)>5 else 'set'\n    name=f\"{set_id}-{item_id}-thumb.jpg\".replace('/','-')\n    media=MediaIoBaseUpload(io.BytesIO(data), mimetype='image/jpeg', resumable=False)\n    f=drive.files().create(body={'name':name,'parents':[FOLDER_ID]}, media_body=media, fields='id,size,webViewLink').execute()\n    uploaded += 1\n    drive.permissions().create(fileId=f['id'], body={'type':'anyone','role':'reader'}, fields='id').execute()\n    # Trash old full-size archived file if this row pointed at one.\n    if len(row) > 13:\n        m=re.search(r'id=([A-Za-z0-9_-]+)', row[13])\n        if m and m.group(1) != f['id']:\n            try:\n                drive.files().update(fileId=m.group(1), body={'trashed': True}).execute()\n                trashed += 1\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n    img_url=f'https://drive.google.com/uc?export=view&id={f[\"id\"]}'\n    formula=f'=IMAGE(\"{img_url}\",4,120,90)'\n    updates.append({'range':f'Selections!N{idx}:O{idx}', 'values':[[img_url, formula]]})\nif updates:\n    sheets.spreadsheets().values().batchUpdate(spreadsheetId=SHEET_ID, body={'valueInputOption':'USER_ENTERED','data':updates}).execute()\nprint('uploaded_lightweight', uploaded, 'trashed_old', trashed, 'avg_bytes', round(bytes_total/max(uploaded,1)), 'total_kb', round(bytes_total/1024,1))\nPY","timeout":300,"workdir":"/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp"}

```text
Agreed — we should not keep heavy photos around for tossed books.

I changed the setup to use **lightweight thumbnails only** for the Google archive.

What I did:
- Replaced the 30 current archived images with tiny thumbnails.
- Trashed the larger Drive copies.
- Updated the Sheet columns to:
  - **Archived Image URL**
  - **Thumbnail**
- Current archived image footprint:
  - **30 thumbnail files**
  - **~119 KB total**
  - **~4 KB per book average**

Going forward:
- When someone makes a selection, the app will:
  1. save the live decision as before
  2. create a small ~320px thumbnail
  3. upload that lightweight thumbnail to the Google Drive folder
  4. write the Sheet row with the thumbnail
- We do **not** need to preserve full crop images long-term in Railway/app storage once they’re logged.
- Railway/app images can be treated as temporary working assets for the current batch.

Deployed:
- Commit: `33b062c`
- Railway deploy: successful
- Tests: **24/24 passing**

Sheet:
https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1Yurn6ACfQDHj-FbwRCmd6pbS_pyXFhTXMNBUJ8w0bOI/edit

### assistant

```text
No — the current Sheet is too database-y. It has useful information, but too many columns for actual human review.

I’d split it into **visible useful columns** vs **hidden audit columns**.

## Keep visible

These are the columns I think should stay visible:

- **Thumbnail**  
  Most important. This is how you know which book it is.

- **Decision**  
  Keep / discard / clear.

- **Reviewer**  
  Useful once Sandra/DJ decisions are coming in live.

- **Timestamp ET**  
  Human-readable enough; no need to show UTC.

- **Category**  
  Maybe useful if you later want to know sticker vs activity vs coloring.

- **Item ID**  
  Useful as a stable reference, but could be renamed to something friendlier like `Book ID`.

- **Set ID**  
  Useful if we process multiple photo batches, but could be renamed to `Batch`.

## Hide or remove from visible view

These are not useful day-to-day:

- **Timestamp UTC**  
  Keep hidden only if we care about audit precision.

- **Device**  
  Mildly useful for debugging, not for sorting.

- **Session ID**  
  Debug-only. Hide.

- **Book Title**  
  Right now it’s just `Book 01`, `Book 02`, etc., so it doesn’t add value. If we later OCR/title books, then it becomes useful.

- **Rotation**  
  Debug-only. Hide.

- **Source Photo**  
  Debug/batch provenance. Hide.

- **App URL**  
  Mostly redundant. Hide.

- **Archived Image URL**  
  Needed behind the scenes for thumbnails, but ugly. Hide.

## Better Sheet layout

I’d make the visible columns:

1. **Thumbnail**
2. **Decision**
3. **Reviewer**
4. **Date**
5. **Batch**
6. **Book ID**
7. **Category**
8. **Notes** — optional, blank for now

Then hide the backend/audit columns after that.

So: yes, keep the data, but make the Sheet look like a simple review log rather than an API export.

(lightweight)\n\n``​`bash\npip install pymupdf pymupdf4llm\n``​`\n\n**Via helper script**:\n``​`bash\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf              # Plain text\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --markdown    # Markdown\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --tables      # Tables\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --images out/ # pages\n``​`\n\n**Inline**:\n``​`bash\npython3 -c \"\nimport pymupdf\ndoc = pymupdf.open('document.pdf')\nfor page in doc:\n    print(page.get_text())\n\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## marker-pdf (high-quality OCR)\n\n``​`bash\n# Check disk space first\npython scripts/extract_marker.py --check\n\npip install marker-pdf\n``​`\n\n**Via helper script**:\n``​`bash\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf                # Markdown\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf --json         # JSON with metadata\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf --output_dir out/  # Save images\npython scripts/extract_marker.py scanned.pdf                 # Scanned PDF (OCR)\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf --use_llm      # LLM-boosted accuracy\n``​`\n\n**CLI** (installed with marker-pdf):\n``​`bash\nmarker_single document.pdf --output_dir ./output\nmarker /path/to/folder --workers 4    # Batch\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Arxiv Papers\n\n``​`\n# Abstract only (fast)\nweb_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/abs/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])\n\n# Full paper\nweb_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])\n\n# Search\nweb_search(query=\"arxiv GRPO reinforcement learning 2026\")\n``​`\n\n## Split, Merge & Search\n\npymupdf handles these natively — use `execute_code` or inline Python:\n\n``​`python\n# Split: extract pages 1-5 to a new PDF\nimport pymupdf\ndoc = pymupdf.open(\"report.pdf\")\nnew = pymupdf.open()\nfor i in range(5):\n    new.insert_pdf(doc, from_page=i, to_page=i)\nnew.save(\"pages_1-5.pdf\")\n``​`\n\n``​`python\n# Merge multiple PDFs\nimport pymupdf\nresult = pymupdf.open()\nfor path in [\"a.pdf\", \"b.pdf\", \"c.pdf\"]:\n    result.insert_pdf(pymupdf.open(path))\nresult.save(\"merged.pdf\")\n``​`\n\n``​`python\n# Search for text across all pages\nimport pymupdf\ndoc = pymupdf.open(\"report.pdf\")\nfor i, page in enumerate(doc):\n    results = page.search_for(\"revenue\")\n    if results:\n        print(f\"Page {i+1}: {len(results)} match(es)\")\n        print(page.get_text(\"text\"))\n``​`\n\nNo extra dependencies needed — pymupdf covers split, merge, search, and text extraction in one package.\n\n---\n\n## Notes\n\n- `web_extract` is always first choice for URLs\n- For large restaurant nutrition PDFs hosted on GetBento/Bento, use `web_search` with `site:getbento.com` + item names if you need exact row snippets or the latest PDF variant before/after `web_extract`\n- pymupdf is the safe default — instant, no models, works everywhere\n- marker-pdf is for OCR, scanned docs, equations, complex layouts — install only when needed\n- Both helper scripts accept `--help` for full usage\n- marker-pdf downloads ~2.5GB of models to `~/.cache/huggingface/` on first use\n- For Word docs: `pip install {"command":"/opt/data/google-accounts/.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom PIL import Image, ImageOps, ImageDraw, ImageFont\nroot=Path('/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp')\nmanifest=json.loads((root/'sample-crops/manifest.json').read_text())\nitems=manifest['items']\noutdir=Path('/tmp/book_ocr_sheets'); outdir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\ntry:\n    font_big=ImageFont.truetype('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf', 28)\n    font_small=ImageFont.truetype('/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf', 18)\nexcept Exception:\n    font_big=font_small=None\nfor page,start in enumerate(range(0,len(items),8), start=1):\n    subset=items[start:start+8]\n    tile_w,tile_h=620,760\n    cols=2; rows=(len(subset)+1)//2\n    sheet=Image.new('RGB',(cols*tile_w, rows*tile_h),'white')\n    draw=ImageDraw.Draw(sheet)\n    for idx,item in enumerate(subset):\n        x=(idx%cols)*tile_w; y=(idx//cols)*tile_h\n        img=Image.open(root/item['src'].lstrip('/')).convert('RGB')\n        rot=item.get('rotation',0)\n        if rot:\n            img=img.rotate(-rot, expand=True, fillcolor='white')\n        img=ImageOps.contain(img,(tile_w-40,tile_h-90), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)\n        sheet.paste(img,(x+(tile_w-img.width)//2,y+60+(tile_h-90-img.height)//2))\n        draw.rectangle([x,y,x+tile_w-1,y+tile_h-1], outline=(0,0,0), width=2)\n        draw.text((x+16,y+12), f\"{item['id']} | {item.get('title','')}\", fill=(0,0,0), font=font_big)\n        draw.text((x+16,y+44), 'Read the largest cover title text', fill=(80,80,80), font=font_small)\n    path=outdir/f'book_ocr_page_{page}.jpg'\n    sheet.save(path, quality=92)\n    print(path)\nPY","timeout":120,"workdir":"/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp"}

### assistant

```text
Done — I added best-effort OCR titles.

What changed:
- Updated the Google Sheet **Title** column from `Book 01`, `Book 03`, etc. to readable titles where possible.
- Updated the app manifest too, so the gallery/contact sheet will use these titles going forward.
- Deployed the app with the OCR-derived titles.

Examples now live:
- `01-01` → **Never Touch a Porcupine!**
- `01-02` → **Dada**
- `01-06` → **A Winter’s Tale**
- `01-14` → **Never Touch a Monster!**
- `01-16` → **The Grouchy Ladybug**
- `01-19` → **But Not the Hippopotamus**
- `01-22` → **Baby Beluga**
- `01-26` → **Peek-a Who?**
- `01-31` → **Good Night Planes**
- `01-33` → **El escúter de Rubén**
